## The Source Base

**49 hand-written references carry the argument and 2,790 harvested records map the field, and this article keeps them apart on purpose.** The designation half of the argument rests on four documents, being the register, the registry's missing-designations page, the registry's page for this vehicle and the governing instruction. **Those are not commentary on the evidence. They are the evidence**, because the question is what a document does and does not contain.

### The Pool

**Three sweeps retrieved 17,071 records, of which 15,522 were distinct.** The shared sweep store removed 1,068 of those, the subject gate admitted 2,897, and 106 fell to duplicate registration, leaving 2,790 in the reference list.

### The Report-Primary Fraction, Which This Pass Was Aimed At

**The report-primary fraction is 19.4 percent, being 540 records of 2,790.** The draft pass measured 9.2 percent, being 226 of 2,452.

**The count rose by 314 and the reference list rose by 338**, so the fraction moved for two reasons at once and the article will not present it as one. **The supplementary sweeps added report primaries, and the gate corrections below removed records that were never on the subject at all.** Non-primary references rose by 24.

**The two preceding articles in this series whose subjects were administrative acts measured 0.0 percent and 2.0 percent, the two lowest of the series** [[X-Planes: X-39, Reserved but Never Assigned][related_post_a336_x39_reserved_never_assigned]] [[X-Planes: X-52, the Designation Refused][related_post_a349_x52_designation_refused]]. **This article's subject is a decision about an aeroplane that exists**, and the aeroplane has a large defence-report literature.

### Report Coverage Is Bought With the Number of Queries and the Sweep Proved It

**The draft asked the space agency's server 12 questions and the later sweeps asked it 42 more, for 54 in all, against 62 put to the defence registry.** The [X-57][related_post_a354_esaero_x57_maxwell] established that the report server reports its true total and serves ten, ignores the offset, and that the fetching library paginates correctly. **So the second sweep asked many narrow questions rather than a few broad ones.**

**The space agency's contribution went from 16 records to 77.** That is the ceiling being bought off rather than lifted, and it is the first time in this series that the prediction has been tested by acting on it.

### A Proper Noun With No Context Requirement Is Not an Anchor

**The pool holds 74 titles containing the word Valkyrie and 1 of them has the subject beside it.** The rest are Wagner, the 1944 Stauffenberg plot, a Tolkien theology paper, an America's Cup yacht, a fuzzing tool, a quadruped robot and **the space agency's own humanoid robot, which is also called Valkyrie**. A bare `Kratos` admitted the Greek personification of power and two Iron Age history papers, the word being ordinary Greek for strength.

**The draft pass shipped all of them**, and the cluster named for this aeroplane reported 75 records of which 74 were noise. **It now reports one.** **The [X-57][related_post_a354_esaero_x57_maxwell] met this through a physicist's surname and recorded it in its store notes**, and the lesson was carried into this article's store and not into its gate, **which is the shape of a lesson learned in the wrong place**.

**And the one record that survives is a finding rather than a disappointment.** It computes a radar cross-section for a computer-aided design model its own title calls Valkyrie-like [[Chung and Tuan 2020][research_maxchung_tuan_2020]]. **The only paper in a 2,680-record survey that studies this aeroplane had to build something that resembles it first.**

### Expendable in Aerospace Means a Rocket

**The attritability cluster held 33 records and about half of them were launch vehicles.** The Evolved Expendable Launch Vehicle, expendable bathythermographs, expendable batteries and expendable infrared sensors between them crowded out the subject. **The word is a homonym of this article's subject and not a broader case of it**, so a launch context now excludes it while an expendable turbojet for an unmanned aircraft still passes.

**The guard failed on its first run because of a letter.** `launch vehicle` written with a word boundary does not match `launch vehicles`, so `Low-cost expendable launch vehicles` walked through a guard written against exactly that phrase. **This is the second time in this article that a plural has defeated an instrument**, the first having refused 24 records on the plural of UAV.

### Two More Homonyms, Both Found by Reading the Kept Sample

**`force` admitted `The Erosion of the Enlisted Force A Study of Attrition`.** The word appears in a large share of the defence registry's titles, so as a context term for combat attrition it guarded nothing. **Personnel turnover is a homonym of combat attrition and not a special case of it**, but `Personnel Attrition Rates in Historical Land Combat Operations` IS the subject, so the cut is by turnover vocabulary rather than by the word personnel.

**And `turbo` admitted a turbocharged diesel engine**, which has a specific fuel consumption like anything else that burns fuel. The anchor now names the turbine engines rather than the stem they share with a compressor on a car.

**What was deliberately not filtered is the learning-curve method literature.** A paper on the microwave oven learning curve is in the pool, and it is there because **the learning curve is a domain-general method this article applies**, which is the precedent the [X-52][related_post_a349_x52_designation_refused] set when it kept edit distance applied to protein surfaces. **A method the article uses brings its literature with it.**

### The Word the Programme Uses Is Not the Word the Field Uses

**Of the 2,790 records in the reference list, the number whose title contains the word attritable is 9.** That is the word the laboratory built the programme around and put in its portfolio name.

**Widening to expendable, disposable and attrition-tolerant reaches 20, and widening again to low cost reaches 121.** **A concept with a programme, an aeroplane and a fact sheet has almost no literature under its own name and a large one under a plainer word.** The preceding three articles each found that a probe written in the author's vocabulary measures the author rather than the field. **This is the same effect with the vocabulary belonging to the Air Force.**

### Two Store Families Were Opened and Both Were Opened Against Records

**The shared sweep store holds patterns observed to contaminate earlier sweeps, each recorded with the incident that produced it.** Applied to this harvest with nothing switched off it removed 1,301 records, and opening the two returned 233 of them. **Two families were then opened by name and the release was measured as a set difference rather than summed from the histogram**, because a record held by a second armed pattern does not return when the first is opened.

**The missile family was opened because this subject sits on the boundary the pattern was written to police.** An attritable aeroplane is priced against a munition, so the comparison the article is made of lives in the missile literature, and the budget documents that carry the procurement numbers are filed under missile procurement. **The pattern's own note anticipates this, saying it is subject-specific and must not be carried blindly.**

**The electrical-machine family was opened because every record it held was electromagnetic aircraft launch**, which is the catapult half of runway independence.

**The medical family stayed armed and it earned its keep in a way worth recording.** The string Xq is the long arm of the human X chromosome, so the designation pulled in karyotype papers, and the name pulled in book reviews, because Valkyrie is a novel as well as an aeroplane. **The [X-57][related_post_a354_esaero_x57_maxwell] met the same effect through a physicist's surname and recorded it. This is the second consecutive article whose aeroplane's name is a homonym.**

### Three Store Patterns Were Repaired, and Every One Was Found by Reading Drops

**A bare curriculum stem was removing reinforcement-learning papers.** Curriculum learning is a training schedule in which an agent is shown easy tasks before hard ones, and the pattern, earned honestly against school timetables, removed three air-combat autonomy papers that are this article's own autonomy cluster. **The pedagogical sense is kept armed and the machine sense is released.**

**A bare ODE stem was removing an aircraft-design paper for using the abbreviation correctly.** The pattern was earned against the parachute problem as a differential-equations exercise and it removed a comparison of Breguet and numerical evaluation of a cruise mission segment. **A title naming Breguet or aircraft design is aeronautical whatever else it says.**

**And a civil-application pattern was removing combat search and rescue.** The guard is the single word in front of it. **All three repairs assert the original incident still drops**, because a guard that releases the record it was written for is a deletion rather than a repair.

### The Gate Was Wrong in Both Directions at Once, Which Is the Failure Only Reading Finds

**Too permissive.** A bare cost-effectiveness anchor admitted breast cancer treatment and computer-based instruction in military training. A bare attrition anchor admitted personnel turnover in explosive ordnance disposal, which is the word meaning something else entirely. A bare specific-fuel-consumption anchor admitted a direct-injection diesel engine. A bare expeditionary anchor admitted a water-quality meter.

**Too narrow, and this one refused the article's own subject on a letter.** The anchors were written with a word boundary after UAV, which does not match UAVs. **26 records were refused on the plural alone**, among them cooperative air combat between high-performance manned fighters and low-performance unmanned aircraft, which is the loyal wingman concept in a title, a pneumatic parachute rescue system, and a vision-based landing system using an inflated airbag. **The first two are this article's central operational idea and the second two are its recovery section.**

**Both corrections were made and both samples were read again.**

### The Cluster Order Was a Measurement and Was Changed Because of It

**The general unmanned-aircraft pattern matches 1,206 of the 2,790 references**, because a sweep about an unmanned aeroplane returns a pool of unmanned aeroplanes. Read before the specific clusters it would claim 1,206 and starve every cluster below it, and as placed it claims 819. **Starving the clusters below is the defect the [X-56][related_post_a353_lockheed_martin_x56] recorded when its own central subject reported 19 where it was 325.** **A class that general is a residual and not a subject**, so it now claims only what the specific clusters did not.

### Where the Displayed Relations Come From

**37 of the 40 display equations carry a citation within six lines above or twelve below.** The remainder are arithmetic on a relation cited immediately above them, which is the same result the [X-46][related_post_a343_boeing_x46] measured at 27 of 37 and recorded rather than closed.

**The number is reported because the alternative is to cite the same source beside every step of a derivation**, which pads a reference list without adding a source. **What would be wrong is a displayed relation whose provenance is nowhere in the section**, and the measurement exists to find that.

### The Report Server Serves Ten Records a Query However the Query Is Phrased

**The space agency's report server contributed 77 records to the reference list across 54 queries.** That is the ceiling and not a measurement of the subject. The [X-57][related_post_a354_esaero_x57_maxwell] established that the server reports its true total and serves ten, and ignores the offset, and that the fetching library sends the offset correctly. **Report coverage is bought with the number of queries and never with the row count.**

**For this subject that ceiling matters less than usual**, because the literature is held by the defence technical information centre rather than by the space agency, and that registry supplied the great majority of the report primaries.

### The Conclusions Were Probed and One Was Harvested For

**10 of 10 conclusions measure thin under the article's own vocabulary and 8 of 10 are covered under the field's.** That is the fourth article running with the same result, and it is a fact about phrasing rather than about the pool.

| Conclusion | As the article words it | As the field words it |
|---|---:|---:|
| a design number is chosen for what it says rather than taken in turn | 0 | 6|
| an attritable aeroplane's value is in the effect and not the airframe | 9 | 152|
| the break-even between a cheap and a costly aeroplane is a cost ratio | 1 | 137|
| a design life longer than attrition allows is wasted structure | 0 | 78|
| an unmanned aircraft requires the vehicle type symbol and usually omits it | 0 | 6|
| a landing canopy cannot be opened at the speed the vehicle arrives | 19 | 281 *|
| runway independence is cheap in mass and expensive in footprint | 0 | 58|
| a published maximum speed and Mach number may be quoted at different altitudes | 1 | 99|
| the claimed unit cost implies a steeper learning curve than airframes achieve | 0 | 56|
| detection range varies as the fourth root of radar cross-section | 0 | 66|

**One thin conclusion was harvested for and it opened.** The claim that a canopy sized for a survivable touchdown cannot be opened at the speed the vehicle arrives measured 16 records, in a pool that already held 231 titles naming a parachute. **A third sweep of thirty queries aimed at opening loads, inflation and reefing took it to 281**, and the launch and recovery cluster from 127 records to 223. **The claim was thin because the pool held parachutes and not their opening**, which is a difference the first two sweeps had no reason to see.

**Two conclusions stay thin and both are about the designation.** A design number chosen for what it says measures six, and the requirement for a vehicle type symbol measures six. **This series has paid twice to learn why.** A341 ran eight queries for designation systems and got Massachusetts tax valuations of 1771 and salmonella serotype naming, and the [X-52][related_post_a349_x52_designation_refused] declined to buy that measurement again. **The designation half of this article rests on four primary documents and not on the survey**, and it would say exactly the same things if the survey were deleted.

### The Book Identifiers Were Verified on Title and on Author

**All 12 resolve to the books the labels name.** The verification was done against the repository search index rather than against the work records, because the work endpoint returns an internal error for records that plainly exist and returns the same error for keys that do not, so it cannot tell a wrong identifier from a dead one.

## Epistemic State

### Historical Fact

**The XQ-58A designation was allocated on 10-Jul-17 to Composite Engineering with the United States Air Force as sponsor** [[DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]]. **The X-57A was allocated on 03-May-16 and the X-59A on 21-Jun-18** [[DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]]. **There is no X-58 row in the register**, and the string appears once in the whole file, in a note saying it does not exist.

**The aeroplane first flew on 5 March 2019 at Yuma Proving Ground** [[XQ-58A Valkyrie demonstrator completes inaugural flight][ref_first_flight]]. **The contract preceding it was awarded in 2016 under the Low Cost Attritable Strike Demonstrator programme, within the Low Cost Attritable Aircraft Technology portfolio** [[Kratos XQ-58 Valkyrie][ref_ds_q58]] [[Factsheet Technology, XQ-58A Valkyrie][ref_afrl_factsheet]].

**The governing instruction requires a basic or modified mission symbol to accompany a vehicle type symbol, at tier T-0, and separately reserves to the allocating office the authority to skip design numbers at discretion** [[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]].

### Verified by Reading the Document

**The register carries 526 allocation rows and 30 of them are X designations.** **15 of those 30 describe an unmanned aeroplane and zero of them carry the Q vehicle type symbol.** Both counts were recomputed from the register by this article's verifier and neither was taken from a secondary source.

**The three gaps in the X run from 44 to 68 are 52, 58 and 67.** **The out-of-sequence Q numbers inside the range the X series occupies are 48, 58, 67, 72 and 73, of which 58, 67, 72 and 73 are X gaps.**

### Analysis

**The rate at which the X series consumes numbers is 1.385 per year over 26 years, so one skipped number costs about 8.7 months of allocation and the sequence has about 666.6 years of headroom.** This is arithmetic on the register's own dates and it assumes the past rate continues, which is an assumption and not a prediction.

**The lift-to-drag ratio the published range requires is 10.43 at a consumption of 0.80 per hour and 13.69 at 1.05 per hour.** The ceiling requires between 7.65 and 12.53 depending on the thrust lapse law assumed. **The two agree to within the width of the assumptions, which is a consistency check and not a measurement of the aeroplane.**

**The booster propellant needed to reach a lift-off speed between 40 and 70 m per second is between 1.77 and 3.1 percent of the maximum mass.** This ignores engine thrust, drag and rail friction along the launch run, all of which reduce the requirement, so it is an upper bound.

**The equation pass added five results and each is arithmetic on published figures.** **The published maximum speed of 566 knots is Mach 0.856 at sea level and Mach 0.987 at the quoted ceiling**, so it agrees with the published Mach 0.85 at the first and not the second. **The two published unit costs, read as a learning curve, imply a progress ratio of 0.5** against the 80 to 85 percent the standard texts quote, and an 85 percent curve would need 4.27 doublings rather than one.

**Once the design life stops binding, the cost per sortie is the unit cost multiplied by the probability of loss, so the derivative with respect to survivability is the unit cost itself.** A percentage point is worth -20,000 dollars a sortie at the lower unit cost. **The break-even against an aeroplane that is not meant to be lost depends only on the ratio of the two unit costs and not on either price.**

**The canopy that gives an eight metre per second touchdown, opened at 60 metres per second, would impose 56.2 times gravity.** **The arithmetic is right and the premise is impossible**, which is why the recovery must be staged, and a first stage of about 0.1778 of the full area holds the load to ten times gravity.

**The cruise model closes against its own input to within two percent**, returning 5,451 kilometres against a published 5,556.

**The primary-reference pass added one result and it is the sharpest cost statement in the article.** A congressional research product records that the Air Force has stated a collaborative combat aircraft would cost roughly one third the price of a crewed fighter [[U.S. Air Force Collaborative Combat Aircraft, CRS IF12740, 15 August 2024][ref_crs_cca_2024]]. **At that cost ratio of three, the break-even relation demands a survival probability of 0.97 against a fighter surviving 0.99.** **The demonstrator's own published unit costs give a ratio of 20 to 40 against an assumed eighty million dollar fighter**, and that assumption is flagged wherever it is used.

### Inference

**That the number 58 was taken from the X series is an inference from three dates and it is the registry's inference before it is this article's.** No allocation letter is public. **The inference is strong and it is not proof.**

**That the Valkyrie's Q designation follows from its being the only unmanned aeroplane in the register to carry the required vehicle type symbol is this article's own inference and it is weaker.** It is consistent with the register and it is not stated anywhere in the record. **Nobody has been found saying it.**

**That a programme expecting to be procured had a reason to prefer a designation that survives the transition to service is a hypothesis and is labelled as one.** The MQ-58B exists and the X-35 became the F-35, so the mechanism is real. **Whether it operated in 2017 is unknown.**

### What the Record Does Not Settle

**Why the letters are XQ rather than X is unclear, and the registry says so in those words** [[Kratos XQ-58 Valkyrie][ref_ds_q58]].

**Whether X in XQ-58A is the status prefix or the basic mission symbol is not stated anywhere consulted.** The sibling designations favour the status prefix and the instruction's own example shape favours the basic mission. **Under the first reading the designation omits a symbol the instruction requires at its highest tier. Under the second it is well formed and its number is still wrong.**

**Whether the engine is a turbojet or a turbofan is not settled by any primary document found here.** The arithmetic favours the turbofan and does not decide it.

**No source states that the maximum speed is a sea level figure.** The article infers it because the alternative puts a subsonic airframe at Mach 0.987. **That is an inference from consistency and not a statement anybody has made.**

**Whether the two published unit costs are cumulative-unit figures or annual-rate figures is not stated**, and the two readings describe different mechanisms. **The article reports both and settles neither.**

**On what basis the payload fraction is quoted is not stated either**, so the comparison with the figure the [X-46][related_post_a343_boeing_x46] measured may be comparing two different things.

**How the parachute recovery is staged is not documented anywhere consulted.** The article derives that it must be staged and does not report how.

**Which loading the published range assumes is not stated**, and the answer changes the implied lift-to-drag ratio by nearly a factor of two.

**What a collaborative combat aircraft will actually cost is not settled by anything consulted.** The service's stated ratio of one third is a statement of intent recorded by a research service, not a price.

**What the aeroplane costs to operate is not published**, so every cost-per-sortie figure in this article is airframe amortisation alone. **The fuel component can be bounded and is under one percent of the amortisation at any plausible price**, which narrows the gap without closing it. **Maintenance, ground equipment, boosters, parachutes and people remain unquantified**, and the fact sheet's claim of low maintenance is the one no public figure supports.

**The radar cross-section is not published**, so the relation between survivability and its price is displayed as a mechanism and never evaluated for this aeroplane.

## Out of Scope

**The tactics of manned and unmanned teaming are not treated here.** The literature is large and the survey below maps it, but the question this article asks is about a designation and an aeroplane, not about how to fight with one.

**The Collaborative Combat Aircraft programme is named and not examined.** It postdates the allocation by years and its own competition, its increments and its contractors belong to a different article. **One number about it is used**, being the cost ratio the service has stated to Congress, and it is used only to put a value into a relation this article derives about the demonstrator. **Whether the programme's economics work is a question this article asks and does not answer.**

**The autonomy software is named and not examined.** What an autonomy package does, how it is tested and whether it can be trusted are questions this article touches only where they bear on why the airframe exists.

**The X-59 is named only as the next allocation in the register.** It is the subject of the next article in this series and nothing is said about it here beyond its date and its designation.

**The classified portion of the designation record is not addressed**, because there is no way to address it. The register is the public record and this article treats it as complete for the numbers it discusses while noting that it is a supplement rather than a full list.

## Conclusion

**The X-58 does not exist, the number 58 does, and the record shows the second without explaining the first.** The registry states that the slot was skipped and that the number was taken from the X series by the Kratos Valkyrie [[Missing USAF and DOD Aircraft Designations][ref_missing_mds]] [[DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]]. **The governing instruction shows how such an absence is permitted, reserving to the allocating office the authority to skip a design number at discretion and requiring no reason to be recorded** [[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]].

**What the record does not contain is the exercise of that authority.** No allocation letter for the X-58 is public, and none is likely to exist, because a number consumed by another series does not need to be refused. **The article therefore says that the absence is permitted and unexplained, rather than that somebody decided it**, and the difference between those two sentences is the whole of what this pass had to repair.

**It went to an aeroplane that flew.** The XQ-58A was allocated fourteen months after the [X-57][related_post_a354_esaero_x57_maxwell] and flew four years before that programme closed without flying at all. **The number the X series gave up was spent better than the number it kept.**

**What the series lost by giving it up was about 8.7 months of numbering out of six centuries of supply**, so scarcity explains nothing and no argument in this article rests on it.

**Every obvious reason for the Valkyrie not being an X-plane fails against the register.** Half the X series is unmanned. One X row is an armed unmanned combat air vehicle. Another was allocated for the same mission the production Valkyrie is intended to fly. **The register refutes the manned test, the combat test and the armament test in turn, and it does so with its own rows.**

**What survives is a distinction the register cannot record.** An X-plane's value sits in its airframe, so the airframe governs the risk. **An attritable aeroplane's value sits in the effect it delivers, and the airframe is a consumable.** The [X-56][related_post_a353_lockheed_martin_x56] stopped short of the boundary it was built to cross because one airframe was left. **The Valkyrie is designed so that the same question never arises.**

**The arithmetic added one thing the prose could not have reached, and it is the argument for attritable aircraft stated without a price.** Once the design life stops binding, the cost per sortie is the unit cost multiplied by the probability of loss. **The break-even against an aeroplane that is not meant to be lost therefore depends only on the ratio of the two costs**, and needs the price of neither.

**The honest way to use that relation is to put the stated number into it rather than a flattering one.** The Air Force has told Congress that a collaborative combat aircraft would cost roughly one third the price of a crewed fighter [[U.S. Air Force Collaborative Combat Aircraft, CRS IF12740, 15 August 2024][ref_crs_cca_2024]]. **At a cost ratio of three, such an aeroplane must survive 0.97 of its sorties to beat a fighter that survives 0.99 of its own.** **That is a fighter's number, not an attritable one.**

**The demonstrator's own published unit costs give a ratio of 20 to 40 against an assumed eighty million dollar fighter**, and at twenty the requirement falls to 0.8. **The relation is the same and the answer is not, because attritability is a property of a price and not of a shape.**

**So the relation says when the case fails as clearly as when it holds**, which is the half a programme brochure leaves out. **The argument needs a hostile sky and a large cost ratio together**, and an aeroplane at a third the price of a fighter, flown where fighters mostly come home, has neither.

**And there is one thing the register does record, which is the shape of a system losing a rule.** Four of the five out-of-sequence Q numbers inside the X range are X gaps, the fifth follows a fighter, and the registry has already written of the fighters that there is to all intents and purposes no longer a sequence [[Missing USAF and DOD Aircraft Designations][ref_missing_mds]]. **A design number was once a position in a queue. It is becoming a thing a programme chooses because of what it says.**

**A first draft of this conclusion called the X-58 the first place in the X series where a number stopped being the next one and became the right one. The register refutes that twice.**

**The registry names the X-50A as a special number**, allocated on 13-Feb-02, 15 years earlier, and this series has already written that article [[Current Designations of U.S. Military Aircraft][ref_mds_aircraft]] [[X-Planes: Boeing X-50 Dragonfly][related_post_a347_boeing_x50]]. **And the XQ-58A is not the first of its own family either**, the XRQ-72A having been allocated on 20-Mar-17, 4 months before it, on a number that is also a gap in the X series [[DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]].

**What is true is narrower and it is the claim the article can support.** The XQ-58A is the earliest allocation whose number the registry states outright was taken from the X series, the 72 being recorded as unclear and the 73 as probable [[Missing USAF and DOD Aircraft Designations][ref_missing_mds]]. **It is the first case where the registry is certain, not the first case.**

**The X-58 is not a missing aeroplane.** It is a number that was spent in another series while it was still the next one due, by an authority that was not obliged to say so and did not.
